The Distance of the lac Regulatory Region to the Translation Start Codon of gfp on Plasmid pEAH23A Does not Change GFP Expression Levels

Trushaan Bundhoo, Adam Abdirahman Hassan, Brandon G. Wong, Rochelle Guan — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.14288/ujemiplus.v10.203919

Bordetella pertussis, the causative agent of whooping cough, uses various strategies to evade host immune defenses, notably through its autotransporter protein Bordetella resistance to killing (BrkA) which blocks complement-mediated killing. Therefore, understanding how brkA expression is regulated is crucial for understanding its role in pathogenesis. Previous research has demonstrated the involvement of the lac regulatory region, located upstream of the brkA locus on the pDO6935 plasmid in brkA expression. When this region was cloned onto a promoterless plasmid, it was able to drive GFP expression. However, the impact of the proximity of regulatory regions on gene expression remains poorly understood. In this study, we investigated how the distance between the lac promoter and a reporter gene, gfp, on a plasmid, impacts GFP expression levels. Utilizing site-directed mutagenesis, plasmids pBRAT24C and pBRAT24D were created, with shorter distances between the lac promoter and gfp relative to pEAH23A. Plasmids were transformed into DH5α Escherichia coli for expression and further analysis. Quantitative GFP analysis showed that pBRAT24C and pBRAT24D induced lower levels of GFP expression than pEAH23A. This suggests that the distance between the lac promoter and gfp does not affect levels of GFP expression and that the sequences found upstream of gfp might affect gene expression through other mechanisms. Further study into the regulatory effects of this region can help maximize BrkA yield from pDO6935, aiding future investigations into its function, structure, and potential as a vaccine candidate.

Measles outbreak propagated by poor isolation practices in schools and health facilities, Mityana District, Uganda, March–September 2025

Maria Nakabuye, Richard Migisha, Nasif Matovu, Justine Wobusobozi, Anne Loy Alupo — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12982-026-03093-5

In April 2025, Mityana District in Central Uganda confirmed a measles outbreak involving one suspected death. We investigated to determine outbreak magnitude, identify transmission risk factors, and assess vaccination coverage (VC) and vaccine effectiveness (VE) to inform control and prevention measures. We defined a suspected case as onset of fever and maculopapular rash with cough, coryza, or conjunctivitis in a Mityana District resident during March–September 2025; a confirmed case was a suspected case with measles IgM positivity. We identified cases through health facility record review and active community case search. We conducted descriptive epidemiology and an age-matched case-control study (1:1) using neighborhood controls. We used conditional logistic regression to estimate odds ratios (ORs) and 95% CIs for factors associated with infection. We estimated VC as the proportion of vaccinated controls and calculated VE as 1-OR MH × 100%. We identified 100 case-patients (5 confirmed, 95 suspected), including one death. The earliest case was a 12-month-old male from Kiboga District, where a measles outbreak was ongoing. The highest attack rates occurred among children aged 9–17 months (11/10,000) and 18–59 months (10/10,000). Receipt of measles-rubella (MR) vaccine dose 1 (MR1) was protective (aOR = 0.24; 95% CI: 0.095–0.53). School attendance (aOR = 3.9; 95% CI: 1.2–12) and a health facility visit within three weeks before illness onset (aOR = 2.6; 95% CI: 1.2–5.7) were associated with increased risk. VE was 67% (95% CI: 31%-85%) for MR1 and 81% (95% CI: 24%-99%) for MR2. VC among controls was 70% for MR1 and 52% for MR2. The outbreak was likely introduced from a neighboring district and sustained by suboptimal MR vaccination coverage and transmission in schools and health facilities. We recommended immediate targeted mass MR vaccination campaigns and strengthened triage and isolation in schools and health facilities to interrupt transmission. To prevent future outbreaks, we recommended strengthening routine immunization, particularly MR2, and enhancing community sensitization and surveillance for early detection and response.

Two Paths of Conversation: How Stories and Statistics are Associated With Community Engagement in Online Debate Surrounding Vaccine Science Skeptical Content

Han Zao Li, Inhwan Bae, Pengfei Zhao, Natalya N. Bazarova, Drew Margolin — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1177/00936502261496144

Statistical and narrative evidence represent two distinct ways of substantiating claims in public reasoning, but does this distinction matter for how people engage with vaccine science skeptical (VSS) content and the discursive spaces surrounding it? This study addresses this question through a large-scale, LLM-assisted observational analysis of online engagement with VSS posts and comments that support or counter such content using statistical versus narrative evidence in the Reddit community r/DebateVaccines . Building on Fisher’s distinction between rational and narrative paradigms , we theorize two paths of conversation through which evidentiary forms are associated with audience engagement with contentious vaccine discourses in a naturalistic social media setting: a rational pathway, marked by concentrated participation and corrective contestation around statistical claims and counterclaims, tending toward a single evidence-based truth “approved” by one-click reactions; and a narrative pathway, characterized by generative engagement featuring broader and more distributed participation, more discursive and generative engagement, and greater acceptance of competing viewpoints. These findings provide a springboard for theorizing evidentiary form as a key epistemic logic associated with how conversations evolve in real-world engagement with contentious, socially consequential issues such as vaccine misinformation, while informing hybrid approaches to addressing skepticism toward established vaccine science.

The current study reports the rational design, systematic optimization and in-depth biological testing of a new composite drug-delivery platform of green-synthesized silica nanoparticles (SiNPs) encapsulated within a PDLLA-PEG-PDLLA (poly(D,Llactide)-poly(ethylene glycol)-poly(D,L-lactide)) triblock thermosensitive hydrogel to locally to TNBC tumor cells. All these results make TRL-SiNP-HG an environmentally friendly, biocompatible, and therapeutically superior platform to be used in the local treatment of TNBC.

In Vitro Anti-Cataract Activity of Hydro-Alcoholic Extract of Pennisetum purpureum (Cenchrus purpureus) Against Sodium Selenite-Induced Cataractogenesis in Isolated Goat Lens

Karthieyan M, V S Saravanan, Karthika A — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.25258/ijddt.16.88s.13

1. Objective The in vitro anti-cataract activity of the hydro-alcoholic extract of Pennisetum purpureum (HAEPP) was assessed in sodium selenite induced isolated goat lenses. 2. Methods The water: Ethanol (1:1 v/v) extracted shade dried whole plant material of Cenchrus purpureus (synonym Pennisetum purpureum) was subjected to physicochemical evaluation (loss on drying, total ash, acid-insoluble ash and watersoluble ash) and the extraction was carried out by continuous hot extraction in a Soxhlet apparatus for 48 hours. The resultant hydro-alcoholic extract (HAEPP) was put to qualitative preliminary phytochemical screening. The anticataract activity was determined by using isolated goat lenses kept in artificial aqueous humor and randomly divided into five groups (n = 5) as follows: normal control; cataract control (sodium selenite 100 μM); reference control (sodium selenite 100 μM + ascorbic acid 10 μg/mL); sodium selenite 100 μM + HAEPP 100 mg/mL; and sodium selenite 100 μM + HAEPP 200 mg/mL. Lenses were graded after 72 hours of incubation for opacity on a semiquantitative scale by photographic means and then total soluble lens protein was estimated by Lowry's method. Oneway ANOVA with Dunnett's t-test was used to analyse the data and a value of p<0.05 was adopted as statistically significant. 3. Results The loss on drying, total ash, acid insoluble ash and water soluble ash of the powdered drug was 1% w/w, 4.2% w/w, 13.5% w/w and 2% w/w respectively and the extractive yield was 14.8% w/w. The phytochemical screening revealed the presence of tannins, saponins, flavonoids, carbohydrate, alkaloids and triterpenoids. Sodium selenite showed dense lenticular opacification (cataract score 3.64 ± 0.24) and significantly reduced the total protein of the lens (1.05 ± 0.01 g/dL) compared with the normal control (cataract score 0; 3.25 ± 0.11 g/dL). Co-incubation with HAEPP produced a dose-dependent reduction in opacity, with cataract scores of 2.62 ± 0.14 (p<0.05) at 100 mg/mL and 1.50 ± 0.07 (p<0.01) at 200 mg/mL, the higher dose approaching the reference standard (1.23 ± 0.08, p<0.001). The total protein content was similarly restored at the two doses, 2.54 ± 0.14 and 3.08 ± 0.16 g/dL, respectively, which were comparable with that of the ascorbic acid reference group (3.21 ± 0.20 g/dL). 4. Conclusion The protection against selenite-induced cataractogenesis seen by HAEPP is dose dependent, likely due to the actions of its individual active components, the alkaloid, flavonoids and saponins, which are thought to inhibit oxidative protein precipitation within the lens. The results suggest that Pennisetum purpureum can be studied for anti-cataract activity using further pharmacological and mechanistic studies. The article provides a detailed report of the anti-cataract activity of the hydro-alcoholic extract of Pennisetum purpureum and Cenchrus purpureus on isolated goat lens with the analysis of phytochemical screening, estimation of total lens protein, oxidative stress, and hydro-alcoholic extract.

Formulation and Optimization of Rizatriptan Benzoate Buccal Tablets for the Management of Acute Migraine

Dhamsheela N. Gondane, Sachidanand Angadi, Anuneet Kaur, Harsha I. Raut, Pravinkumar M. Shahare - — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.25258/ijddt.16.88s.23

The present study aimed to formulate and optimize an efficient mucoadhesive buccal tablet of Rizatriptan benzoate using a 3² design to avoid first-pass hepatic metabolism and also to deliver the drug rapidly at an optimized rate over a prolonged period through the transmucosal route. Validation of the drug through organoleptic evaluation, solubility studies, melting point studies, method development by UV spectrophotometry, drug identification, drug purity, and polymer compatibility studies using FTIR and DSC with specified polymers confirmed drug identification, drug purity, and polymer compatibility, respectively. Nine buccoadhesive tablets were prepared by direct compression and subsequently optimized statistically by using 3² factorial designs to formulate nine batches of buccal tablets by fixing the concentrations of HPMC K15M and chitosan as variables, where the dependent variables were the cumulative drug release at 6 h (CDR) and the mucoadhesive strength. Flow and all pharmacopoeial values of compression were evaluated. The flow of all nine formulations was observed to be in an acceptable range, and all the pharmacopoeial values of compression were maintained satisfactorily. F5 (HPMC K15M 75 mg + chitosan 75 mg) formulation was regarded as the best formulation giving drug content of 99.36 ± 0.24, surface pH of 6.83 ± 0.03, swelling index of 69.30 ± 0.89% at 8 h, highest mucoadhesive strength of 8.56 ± 0.35 gm, adhesive force of 0.84 ± 0.034 N among nine batches, maximum cumulative in vitro drug release of 76.69 ± 0.98% and permeation of 83.74 ± 1.02% at 6 h. The comparison of the dissolution profile of F5 with marketed Rizatriptan benzoate tablet showed an acceptable and comparable release profile, with a model-independent difference (f1 = 13.92) and similarity factors (f2 = 51.54). F5 formulation was found to be physically and chemically stable up to 3 months of accelerated storage (40 ± 2 °C/75% RH). It is therefore concluded that the mucoadhesive buccal tablet offers a promising delivery system for Rizatriptan benzoate, which might act as a statistically optimized, alternative, and feasible dosage form in the acute management of migraine.

Mechanistic understanding of ivermectin and diclofenac removal from Pinus elliottii biochar modified by niobium deposition

Ana Carolina Ferreira Piazzi Fuhr, Taghreed F. Altamimi, Adelício Lucas Alberto, Salah M. El-Bahy, Fernando Machado Machado — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s43153-026-00731-y

Abstract This study investigated the effect of niobium (Nb) surface modification of biochar via magnetron sputtering on the adsorption of ivermectin and diclofenac potassium, using an integrated approach combining equilibrium experiments and statistical physics-based adsorption models. The biochar was obtained by pyrolysis and then coated with Nb for 30 min at 150 W DC power. We analyzed adsorption equilibrium data using advanced isotherm models to evaluate the process’s steric and energetic parameters. The results indicated predominantly physisorption, with adsorption energies of 17–19 kJ mol − 1 for ivermectin and approximately 11 kJ mol − 1 for diclofenac. Steric parameters exceeded 1 for both drugs, suggesting multi-molecular adsorption, while modeling indicated the formation of two layers on the material’s surface. For ivermectin, maximum adsorption capacities ranged from 81 to 111 mg g − 1 . Furthermore, increasing temperature enhanced ivermectin’s affinity for the modified biochar, whereas diclofenac showed the opposite behavior, highlighting distinct adsorption responses between the two contaminants. Based on the modeling parameters and the adsorbent’s physicochemical characteristics, the results suggest that ivermectin adsorption may involve dipole–dipole interactions, hydrogen bonding, and π–π interactions, while diclofenac adsorption may involve electrostatic interactions, hydrogen bonding, and π–π interactions. These results show that Nb surface modification distinctly influences the adsorption behavior of the two drugs and highlight the potential of magnetron sputtering as a surface-engineering strategy for developing biochars with differential affinity for pharmaceutical contaminants.

Impact of matched samples on domain harmonization in cross-cohort NMR metabolomics

Aigar Ottas, L. Goldoni, A. Armirotti, S. Decherchi — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s11306-026-02543-0

INTRODUCTION: Machine learning models trained on metabolomics data frequently show reduced performance when applied to independent cohorts because of domain shifts introduced by differences in analytical platforms, sample handling, storage conditions, and metabolite quantification procedures. Robust approaches for harmonizing metabolomics datasets across cohorts are therefore needed to improve model transferability and reproducibility. OBJECTIVES: To evaluate the impact of matched samples on domain harmonization and cross-cohort prediction performance in NMR metabolomics data, and to compare multiple harmonization strategies for improving model generalization across independently acquired datasets. METHODS: A machine-learning framework was developed to classify ICD-10 code I50.9 (unspecified heart failure) using plasma NMR metabolomics data from the Estonian Biobank. Models were trained on a source dataset comprising 12,000 samples measured at Nightingale Health and evaluated on an independently measured destination dataset of 295 samples acquired on a different NMR platform. Harmonization approaches included within-domain normalization, Optimal Transport, Bridge mapping, and Empirical Bayes correction. The effect of matched samples was assessed using up to 108 duplicated biospecimens measured in both datasets. Additional validation was performed using independently generated human gestational diabetes and mouse diabetes metabolomics datasets from Metabolomics Workbench. RESULTS: Domain harmonization generally improved cross-cohort prediction compared with unaligned data, particularly when matched samples were available. Predictive performance increased as the number of matched samples used for calibration increased, although gains varied across methods. Nevertheless, simple within-domain normalization consistently provided strong performance and often matched or exceeded more complex alignment approaches. External validation using fully unmatched human and mouse metabolomics datasets demonstrated that harmonization strategies improved classification performance relative to raw data, supporting the broader applicability of the framework. CONCLUSION: Matched samples substantially enhance domain harmonization and improve the transferability of metabolomics prediction models across analytical platforms and cohorts. However, robust normalization remains a strong baseline, and the benefits of more sophisticated alignment methods depend on the availability and representativeness of matched reference samples. These findings provide practical guidance for developing reproducible cross-cohort metabolomics machine-learning models while preserving biologically relevant signals.

A regulator-based immunoscore predicts prognosis and immunotherapy response in gastric cancer

Xueliang Tu, Huanhuan Su, Shushen Ji, Changming Wang, Lin Chen — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12957-026-04609-8

Immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) have improved treatment outcomes in gastric cancer (GC); however, only a subset of patients benefit due to tumor heterogeneity. Current biomarkers have limited predictive value, highlighting the need for improved stratification strategies. We analyzed RNA-seq and clinical data from 375 TCGA-STAD patients to identify immune infiltration subtypes using CIBERSORT and consensus clustering, followed by differential expression analysis to obtain 1,454 DEGs. A transcription factor-gene regulatory network was reconstructed using ARACNe, and regulatory protein activity of candidate regulators was inferred with VIPER. Key prognostic regulators were selected through LASSO regression and survival analysis, leading to four master regulators (MYB, NFE2L3,SETBP1,TFAP2A) that were integrated into a PCA-based Immunoscore model. The model was validated in an independent cohort of 45 advanced gastric cancer patients treated with PD-1 inhibitors. Four immune infiltration subtypes with distinct immune cell compositions were identified. Four master regulators (MYB, NFE2L3, SETBP1, TFAP2A) were incorporated into the Immunoscore model. Patients with a high Immunoscore showed significantly improved overall survival in the TCGA cohort ( p = 0.00042). Validation in the PD-1 cohort further confirmed its predictive value ( p = 0.00459), achieving an AUC of 0.838. Comparative benchmarking against PREDICTIO and TIDE demonstrated comparatively favorable predictive performance of the ImmunoScore model for immunotherapy response. Functional enrichment analysis revealed that the model was primarily associated with immune response, inflammatory response, and chemokine signaling pathways. This study presents a regulator-based Immunoscore that is associated with prognosis and immunotherapy response in gastric cancer. Despite limitations, including a small validation cohort and reliance on public datasets, the model offers insights into immune-related regulatory mechanisms and may serve as a potential tool for patient stratification.

Acute myeloid leukemia (AML) is the most common acute leukemia in adults, characterized by marked biological heterogeneity and an unfavorable prognosis. tRNA-derived small RNAs (tsRNAs) are involved in tumor progression, but their roles in AML remain poorly understood, so we investigated tRF-Gln-CTG-7 in AML. tsRNA microarray screening was used to analyze differential expression between AML patients and healthy controls and tRF-Gln-CTG-7 was identified for further investigation. Ethan Clements, Gabriella Pflederer, Lena Shehayber — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.14288/ujemiplus.v10.203921

Malaria is a febrile disease caused by parasites of the genus Plasmodium. The most severe cases and most of the deaths of malaria are due to Plasmodium falciparum infection. Investigating transcriptional differences in P. falciparum between severe and uncomplicated cases can shed light on malaria pathogenicity and virulence. In this study we used previously acquired publicly available RNA sequencing data from severe and uncomplicated cases of P. falciparum infection. Differential expression analysis of the P. falciparum transcriptome showed that a group of gene products involved in merozoite egress and erythrocyte invasion were significantly upregulated in severe malaria. Transcriptome-guided identification of YALI0_F02013g and YALI0_A19910g as candidate NADPH-dependent oxidoreductases associated with enhanced erythritol production in Yarrowia lipolytica

Muhammad Sarfaraz Iqbal, Muhammad Hasnain Iqbal, Tariq Babakarkhil, Abdulrahman Faisal Alkhanani, Wael A. Alsubhi — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13068-026-02827-6

NADPH competition by non-erythrose-reductase oxidoreductases limits erythritol yields in Yarrowia lipolytica , yet the specific contributors remain poorly defined. This study characterised two candidate genes, YALI0_F02013g (putative pyridoxine 4-dehydrogenase) and YALI0_A19910g (putative glycerol 2-dehydrogenase), using time-resolved transcriptomics, molecular docking and metabolic modelling. RNA-seq analysis across 96 h of fermentation revealed distinct temporal profiles: YALI0_F02013g was progressively induced 3.4-fold (peak 5040 FPKM at 72 h), while YALI0_A19910g was repressed 920-fold by 96 h (Table 2). Expression trends were validated by qRT-PCR (Pearson r > 0.94; Table 3). YALI0_A19910g expression correlated strongly negatively with erythritol titre (Pearson r = − 0.858), whereas YALI0_F02013g showed a weak positive correlation. Molecular docking predicted high NADPH binding affinities for both enzymes (− 9.8 and − 8.6 kcal mol⁻ 1 , respectively; Fig. 2) and identified conserved catalytic triads. An integrated prioritisation model ranked YALI0_F02013g as the higher-priority target (5.6/10 vs 5.0/10; Table 7). In silico flux estimates suggest that repressing YALI0_F02013g could conserve approximately 67% of the competing NADPH demand, potentially increasing erythritol yield by ~14%; these projections require experimental validation (Table 8). These findings identify YALI0_F02013g as a candidate metabolic bottleneck in the strain background examined, offering a specific target for engineering improved cofactor efficiency in industrial Yarrowia lipolytica strains.

A CRC–MYB chromatin loop controls auxin-mediated nectary growth in Arabidopsis

Hideaki Iimura, Makoto Takahashi, Ryosuke Ohta, Asumi Nagamori, Seika Togawa — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-78943-6

Abstract Floral nectaries, typically located at stamen bases, secrete sugar-rich nectar containing diverse secondary metabolites. Despite their defined position and specialized function, the mechanisms governing nectary formation remain unclear. Using publicly available single-nucleus RNA-seq data and newly generated spatial transcriptomic data, we identify a putative ordering of transcriptional states associated with nectary development and a spatial regulatory network controlling nectary growth in Arabidopsis thaliana . We further show that CRABS CLAW (CRC) is central to this network, integrating transcriptional regulation with spatial auxin transport and pathways controlling phytohormone signaling, sugar transport and terpene biosynthesis. CRC interacts with MYB21, MYB24 and MYB57 to form a complex that binds the 5′ and 3′ regulatory regions of MACCHI-BOU 4 ( MAB4 ), promoting a chromatin loop that enhances MAB4 expression and supports proper nectary size. MAB4 reinforces PIN-FORMED 1-mediated auxin transport to establish auxin maxima at the nectary tip and regulates PIN6 localization to maintain auxin gradients. These findings link transcriptional regulation and auxin transport with nectary organogenesis and metabolic function.

Oxidative stress-related signature predicts biochemical recurrence and identifies LAMP5 as a candidate prognostic biomarker in prostate cancer

Peiqiang Wu, Jianlei Zhang, Li Guo, B. Chen, Lingxiao Xiong — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12894-026-02411-y

Oxidative stress (OS) has been implicated in the malignant progression of prostate cancer (PCa). Biochemical recurrence (BCR) in PCa patients is closely associated with OS. This study aimed to develop a BCR risk predictive model for PCa by screening OS-related biomarkers and preliminarily validating their roles in PCa progression. RNA-seq data from 495 PCa samples and 151 normal prostate tissues were obtained from The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) and Genotype-Tissue Expression (GTEx) databases to identify differentially expressed genes (DEGs) between tumor and normal tissues. Single-cell RNA-seq data from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) were analyzed using the R package "Seurat" for cell type identification, and OS response-related genes combined with the "AUCell" package were applied to screen OS-active subgroups and their marker genes. The intersection genes between DEGs and OS-active subgroup markers were used to construct a Biochemical Recurrence Risk Score (BCRRS) model through LASSO regression analysis, which was subsequently validated and evaluated for its predictive value. Random forest analysis and immune checkpoint gene correlation analysis were conducted to identify target genes, followed by in vitro experiments to examine the effects of targeted knockdown on PCa cell proliferation, migration, and invasion. The BCRRS model demonstrated reliable and stable predictive value for BCR in PCa patients, with potential differences in tumor-infiltrating immune cell composition observed between high- and low-risk groups. LAMP5 was identified as a critical predictor of BCR in PCa, and its knockdown markedly decreased the proliferation, migration, and invasion abilities of PC3 and DU145 cells. In conclusion, the BCRRS model constructed based on OS-related biomarkers exhibits robust predictive value, and LAMP5 is a candidate prognostic biomarker whose siRNA-mediated knockdown was associated with reduced proliferation, migration, and invasion in PC3 and DU145 cells.

Integrative transcriptomic analysis reveals tumor-adjacent mucosal remodeling in colorectal cancer

Sha Wu, Long Wu, Ruoshi Li, Xin Luo, Xueni Zhao — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s10142-026-02066-w

Tumor-adjacent mucosa is commonly used as a normal reference in colorectal cancer (CRC) transcriptomic studies, but its relationship with distant normal mucosa and tumor tissue remains unclear. Here, we performed bulk RNA sequencing of paired distant normal, adjacent, and tumor tissues from 25 patients with CRC and used these three tissue states to construct an ordered normal–adjacent–tumor (N-A-T) expression continuum. Although adjacent mucosa was globally closer to distant normal mucosa and showed no significant differential expression by pairwise analysis, a limma-based linear trend model identified widespread monotonic transcriptional changes along the N-A-T continuum. Tumor-directed genes were enriched in translation, RNA processing, cell-cycle activity, and proliferation-related pathways, whereas genes decreasing along this axis were associated with oxidative metabolism and epithelial homeostasis. A trend gene-based N-A-T score positioned adjacent mucosa between normal and tumor tissues in external bulk cohorts and was associated with proliferative and immune-low molecular states in CRC. Single-cell RNA-seq analysis localized this signal mainly to epithelial cells and associated higher scores with proliferative and stem-like epithelial states. In conclusion, CRC-adjacent mucosa is not fully equivalent to distant normal mucosa but carries coordinated tumor-directed transcriptional changes mainly associated with epithelial-cell programs.

Single‑cell and bulk RNA sequencing identifies T cell marker genes to predict the prognosis of ovarian cancer

Hengzi Sun, Xiao Huo, Shuhong Li, Liyuan Guo — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-76622-6

Ovarian cancer, with high mortality and often late diagnosis, shows high recurrence despite treatment. The variable effectiveness of immunotherapy highlights the urgent need for personalized, advanced therapeutic strategies. To investigate T-cell marker genes, single-cell RNA-sequencing (scRNA-seq) data were sourced from the Gene Expression Omnibus (GEO) database. Additionally, bulk RNA-sequencing data along with clinical information from ovarian cancer patients were retrieved from the Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) database to establish a prognostic signature. This study involved survival analysis to evaluate associations between different risk groups, and explored cellular communication and relevant pathway analyses, including metabolic pathways. We identified 41 genes showing varied expression between two T-cell subclusters, marking subcluster 0 with CCL5 and GZMA, and attributing the rest to subcluster 1. Re-annotation using canonical T-cell lineage and exhaustion markers confirmed subcluster 0 as activated cytotoxic CD8+T cells (CCL5+GZMA+), while subcluster 1 showed an immediate-early/stress-response gene signature rather than a canonical exhausted phenotype. These markers delineate four prognostic groups within the TCGA OV dataset, with T-cluster 2 exhibiting the poorest survival, in contrast to T-cluster 3, which shows the best. We developed a six-gene T-cell scoring model (DUSP2, ATF3, NR4A1, RGS2, RGS1, TNFAIP3), with the low-score group showing significantly better overall survival (HR 0.74, 95% CI 0.55–0.99, P = 0.048) and progression-free survival (HR 0.69, 95% CI 0.51–0.93, P = 0.012). Moreover, cellular and metabolic pathway analyses underscored the importance of CCL, IL2 and MGMT pathways in these subclusters. The study identifies CCL5 as a biomarker for T-cell subtypes in ovarian cancer using scRNA-seq and bulk RNA-seq data. A T-cell scoring model based on six genes predicts survival and progression rates, and provides incremental prognostic information beyond conventional clinicopathological factors (multivariable Cox: OS HR = 1.32, P = 0.028; PFS HR = 1.38, P = 0.009). This model could be a candidate prognostic indicator and CCL5 is a candidate biomarker and potential therapeutic target that warrants further functional validation in ovarian cancer.

Deletion of a rheumatoid arthritis risk enhancer in mouse disrupts Bim expression and immune homeostasis

Stefano Rossi, Kelly S. Hayes, Jake Butler, James Ding, Ellie Richards — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ero.2026.100260

Objectives This study aimed to characterise the in vivo function of a murine regulatory region syntenic to the human rheumatoid arthritis (RA)-associated enhancer at the ACOXL locus and its role in immunity. Methods Using CRISPR-Cas9, we generated the Bim-eΔ mouse model by excising a 15 kb genomic region syntenic to the human RA-associated locus tagged by rs13401811 from C57BL/6J zygotes. Bim expression was assessed by quantitative polymerase chain reaction. Thymocytes and splenocytes were profiled by flow cytometry, and whole-spleen RNA-seq was used to define transcriptional changes. Immune responses were tested using a Trichuris muris infection model and an antigen-induced arthritis (AIA) protocol. Results Bim-eΔ mice show reduced Bim expression vs wild-type counterparts. This indicates that Bim is a transcriptional target of this region. Enhancer loss was correlated with differences in T cell proportions across age- and sex-stratified cohorts consistent with altered thymic selection. Splenic T and B cells were altered, and the transcriptome skewed toward inflammation. Bim-eΔ and wild-type mice mounted comparable responses to T. muris , whereas Bim-eΔ mice developed a severe adverse response during the sensitisation phase of the AIA protocol. Conclusions A murine noncoding regulatory region modulates Bim expression and immunity in vivo . Enhancer deletion altered the splenic transcriptional profile and lymphocyte composition while revealing a context-dependent phenotype upon inflammatory in vivo challenge. These findings support a role for RA-associated regulatory architecture in shaping immunity and point to BIM regulation as a potential therapeutic lever for restoring immune balance.

ESMF: Elastic Signature-based Matrix Factorization for Robust Cellular Deconvolution

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Shuchao Li, Yunwen Mou, Bingjie Yuan, Jinting Guan, Guoli Ji — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13040-026-00612-7

While bulk RNA sequencing offers clinical practicality through cost-efficiency and scalability for large cohort profiling, it obscures cellular heterogeneity critical for characterizing tissue microenvironment. Conversely, single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) resolves cellular diversity at unprecedented resolution but faces translational barriers including technical complexity and tissue processing artifacts. These complementary yet constrained technologies necessitate computational frameworks that leverage scRNA-seq data insights to enhance bulk data interpretation. Current deconvolution methods, however, remain challenged by technical noise amplification, biological implausibility, and limited rare-cell detection across diverse platforms. We developed ESMF (Elastic Signature-based Matrix Factorization), a deconvolution framework addressing these gaps through: (1) elastic expression signature-based regularization incorporating gene expression distribution parameters (mean/variance) from scRNA-seq data references, (2) negative binomial modeling of technical noise, and (3) dual-validation of marker selection integrating literature-curated knowledge with computational screening. Validated across diverse synthetic and biological datasets using a multi-tiered framework, ESMF demonstrated competitive performance against established benchmarks, exhibiting improved cross-platform stability, enhanced rare-cell detection in clinically relevant samples, and biologically plausible expression estimates. ESMF effectively bridges bulk and single-cell paradigms by resolving cellular proportions and expression profiles with biological fidelity, providing a robust analytical platform for translational tissue microenvironment investigation.

scDAEC: deep clustering of single-cell data based on dual autoencoder

Yue Zhu, Chen Chen, Yue Liu, 康艳蕾, Hancan Zhu — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12864-026-13261-1

Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) provides high-resolution transcriptomic profiles for dissecting cellular heterogeneity, but accurate clustering remains difficult because gene-expression matrices are sparse, noisy, and affected by dropout events. Existing deep clustering models often emphasize either gene-expression reconstruction or cell–cell structural learning, which may limit their ability to jointly account for gene-expression sparsity and preserve cellular topology. Here, we propose scDAEC, a dual-autoencoder framework for unsupervised clustering of scRNA-seq data. scDAEC applies an adaptive zero-inflated negative binomial (ZINB) based denoising autoencoder (DAE) to model gene-expression uncertainty, combined with a graph autoencoder (GAE) to learn topology-aware cell representations. A dynamic combined multi-head attention (DCMHA) module then integrates denoised gene-expression embeddings and graph-based embeddings for clustering optimization. Experiments on 13 real scRNA-seq datasets showed that scDAEC achieved improved clustering accuracy and stable performance compared with other representative methods. Based on the dataset-level mean results, scDAEC achieved overall mean Adjusted Rand Index (ARI) and Normalized Mutual Information (NMI) values of 0.864 and 0.859, respectively. Compared with the strongest competing methods, scDAEC improved the average ARI and NMI by 10.79% and 10.96%, respectively. The source code is available at https://github.com/chanDaniel-cc/scDAEC .

CD147 drives keloid formation by promoting fibroblast proliferation via the CENPF signaling pathway

Jian‐Sheng Diao, Xin-Nan Han, DU Hui-cong, Juan Wu, Jing Jia — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isci.2026.117927

Keloids are benign dermal tumors, and keloid fibroblasts (KFs) play a critical role in their formation through hyperproliferation. However, the mechanism underlying this excessive fibroblast growth in keloids remains elusive. Here, we demonstrate that CD147 is upregulated in KFs and promotes their pathological activities, while its inhibition reverses these abnormalities. Integrated miRNA–mRNA network analysis identifies a miR-15a-5p–centered angiogenic program and biomarker-linked regulatory modules in ovarian cancer

Cleo Sarmiento, Wesley A. Tom, Chao Jiang, Dinesh S. Chandel, Rama Krishna Thelagathoti — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13048-026-02275-8

Ovarian cancer remains the most lethal gynecologic malignancy, in part because it is often diagnosed at an advanced stage, molecular heterogeneity complicates therapeutic selection, and actionable regulatory dependencies remain incompletely defined. MicroRNAs (miRNAs) are well-established post-transcriptional regulators of tumor phenotypes, but most ovarian cancer studies have evaluated either mRNA or miRNA dysregulation in isolation and have therefore provided limited insight into how coordinated miRNA–mRNA programs are organized at the network level. Here we integrated bulk RNA-seq and matched NanoString miRNA profiling from 27 matched cell-line-derived samples, comprising 21 ovarian cancer cell-line samples and 6 control cell-line samples, to define differentially expressed genes and miRNAs, infer regulatory edges, identify community structure, and prioritize biologically coherent modules. RNA-seq identified 7,961 genes at false discovery rate (FDR) < 0.05, of which 2,932 remained strict differentially expressed genes after imposing |log2 fold-change| ≥ 1. miRNA profiling identified 40 significant miRNAs, with a strongly asymmetric landscape dominated by downregulation (35 down, 5 up). Integration of 24 miRNAs with 1,236 mRNAs yielded 4,614 expression-inferred miRNA–mRNA edges after filtering for negative matched-sample Spearman correlation (cor < -0.4; Benjamini–Hochberg adjusted cor_fdr < 0.05), followed by Leiden community detection that resolved five modules. Module numbers represent arbitrary Leiden community identifiers and do not imply biological rank or priority. Two modules showed Benjamini–Hochberg significant Gene Ontology enrichment: an extracellular-matrix/developmental module and an angiogenesis-associated module. The latter was centered on hsa-miR-15a-5p, which was connected by inferred regulatory edges to 109 of 119 genes in Module 5 and was linked to an angiogenic leading-edge set including ADGRG1, ANGPTL4, EREG, HTATIP2, ID1, PTK2, RAPGEF3, SYK, and TNFAIP2. In the discovery dataset, miR-15a-5p was downregulated, whereas the corresponding Module 5 angiogenesis-linked genes were strongly upregulated. In 420 matched TCGA-OV primary tumors, the Module 5 angiogenesis gene set showed a modest positive association with miR-15a-5p, suggesting that the bulk-tumor signal may reflect cellular composition, indirect regulation, or broader angiogenic tumor-state biology rather than simple one-to-one inverse miRNA-mediated repression. Biomarker triage further concentrated priority on epithelial/tumor-associated genes including EPCAM, ESRP1, ST14, TM4SF18, RAB25, and ADRA2C, with hsa-miR-140-5p, hsa-miR-15a-5p, and hsa-miR-381-3p emerging as recurrent regulators. Together, these results define an ovarian-cancer regulatory architecture in which a miR-15a-5p–centered angiogenic program coexists with biomarker-linked epithelial modules, offering a systems-level framework for mechanistic follow-up and translational prioritization.

FOXO3 induces chromatin changes in a model of DNA damage-induced senescence

Abigail K. Brown, José A. Arevalo, Angelina Holcom, Adele Elizabeth Finch, Sun Y. Maybury‐Lewis — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-026-11174-z

FOXO transcription factors are critical for cellular homeostasis and the stress response and have been implicated in longevity in several species. Yet, how these factors function at the chromatin level in differing conditions in human cells is poorly understood. Here, we take an integrated multi-omics approach to identify the chromatin-level mechanisms by which FOXO3 coordinates transcriptional programs in replicating cells and in a model of cellular senescence. In a DNA-damage induced senescence, FOXO3 levels are elevated early in senescence entry. Increased FOXO3 expression induces expression of chromatin remodeling factors, and impacts chromatin accessibility across the genome together with SWI/SNF chromatin remodelers. As cells enter DNA-damage induced senescence, FOXO3 is essential for chromatin remodeling and reprogramming of a genomic network that functions to modulate cell stress and lineage-specific cellular functions. Altogether, we identify a FOXO3-SWI/SNF network that regulates key cellular processes including metabolism, proteostasis, epigenetics, and cell lineage, and is dynamically controlled under DNA damaging conditions.

Decoding radiation-induced transcriptomic signatures of human leukocytes using long-read RNA-seq: clinical and biodosimetric implications

Ahmed Salah, Heinz Schmidberger, Fédérico Marini, Sebastian Zahnreich — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12864-026-13475-3

Abstract Background Gene expression profiling in radiation-exposed blood is a valuable tool for biodosimetry and clinical research. Evaluating the blood’s transcriptomic radiation response provides insight into absorbed dose, hematotoxicities, and immune reactions. However, detailed analysis using long-read RNA sequencing is currently limited in its diffusion, despite the potential additional insights that could be extracted, including novel isoform discovery and on-the-field gene expression studies, owing to its portability. Results In this study, we utilized Oxford Nanopore Technologies’ long-read RNA sequencing on human peripheral blood leukocytes from three healthy donors 6 h after exposure of whole peripheral blood to 4 Gy of X-rays. Compared to sham-irradiated (0 Gy) blood samples, gene-level differential expression analysis identified 117 upregulated and 66 downregulated genes, including canonical DNA damage repair and inflammatory responses. At the transcript level, 102 transcripts were significantly upregulated, and 17 were downregulated, revealing isoform-specific regulation that was not captured at the gene level. Notably, IL32 , which was not detected as significantly changed at the gene level, exhibited significant upregulation of two transcript isoforms (log2 fold-changes of 1.19 and 1.99), while WDR74 , ITM2B , AK2 , and RPS19 displayed changes in transcript usage following irradiation. Leveraging long-read RNA sequencing, we further identified novel candidate isoforms, including protein-coding isoforms of PHPT1 and FBXO22 as well as non-coding candidates. Notably, the FBXO22 isoform showed evidence of altered protein domain architecture relative to the reference isoform. Conclusions This study is the first to systematically investigate differential transcript usage in the leukocyte fraction of irradiated human whole blood using long-read RNA sequencing. We confirmed known radiation-response signatures at the gene level and identified transcript-level regulatory events and novel candidate isoforms that can be masked in gene-level analyses and conventional short-read pipelines. These findings demonstrate the potential of long-read RNA sequencing to provide a more detailed view of radiation-induced transcriptomic alterations, with potential applications in biodosimetry and clinical settings.

Decomposing multi-scale dynamic regulation from single-cell multiomics with scMagnify

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Xufeng Chen, Xi Yan, Bihan Shen, Heqi Wang, Z Tang — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41467-026-78441-9

Deciphering the highly coupled regulatory circuits that drive cellular dynamics remains a fundamental goal in biology. However, capturing the multi-scale time-lagged dynamics and combinatorial regulatory logic of gene regulation remains challenging. Here we present scMagnify, a deep-learning-based framework that leverages chromatin accessibility and gene expression via Granger causality to reconstruct and decompose multi-scale gene regulatory networks (GRNs). Diverse benchmarks demonstrate scMagnify’s favorable performance. scMagnify employs tensor decomposition to identify combinatorial TF modules and their activation profiles across multiple time-lags. We applied scMagnify to human hematopoiesis and mouse pancreas development, where it recovered known lineage-driving regulators and provided further insights into the combinatorial logic underlying cell fate decisions. Furthermore, in the context of kidney injury, scMagnify’s intercellular communication module mapped key signaling-to-transcription cascades linking microenvironment cues to pathological epithelial cell changes. In summary, scMagnify provides a robust computational framework for dissecting the multi-scale regulatory logic that governs complex biological processes in development and disease. Chen and colleagues present scMagnify, a framework that uses single-cell multi-omic data to reconstruct and decompose time-lagged gene regulatory networks. The study analyses regulatory programs associated with development and disease.

Single-cell RNA sequencing of the Tumor Microenvironment in Gynaecological Cancer: Cellular Heterogeneity, Computational Approaches and Clinical Applications

Neha Bonagiri, Elamathi Natarajan — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64823/ijor.2601004

Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) has emerged as a powerful technological advancement, overcoming the primary limitation of bulk-RNA sequencing–its inability to capture cellular heterogeneity. Consequently, scRNA-seq plays a pivotal role in characterising the complex cellular landscape of the tumor microenvironment. Composed of diverse populations, including immune and stromal cells, and characterised by intricate cell-to-cell interactions, the tumor microenvironment is a central driver of tumorigenesis. Examining these dynamic biological systems at the transcriptomic level enables detailed mapping of intercellular communications and facilitates the discovery of targeted therapeutic strategies. This review outlines the end-to-end scRNA-seq workflow, covering fundamental single-cell isolation methods, downstream computational analyses, and key analytical software, as well as their practical significance. Additionally, it highlights the integration of spatial transcriptomics, which provides crucial positional context to achieve a more comprehensive understanding of intra-tumoral heterogeneity. Special emphasis is placed on gynaecological malignancies, particularly cervical and endometrial cancers. Given the rising incidence of both malignancies in recent years, detailed characterisation of their specific microenvironments is critical for advancing diagnostic accuracy and therapeutic intervention. A comprehensive understanding of the progression of the cancer and drug resistance is critical to understand. Accordingly, key molecular alterations, potential gene targets, and emerging biomarkers are systematically reviewed. Finally, current technical constraints, recent technological advancements, and future perspectives for single-cell transcriptomics are evaluated. Keywords: Keywords: Cell-Cell communication; Cervical cancer; Endometrial cancer; Spatial transcriptomics; Tools

Longitudinal single-cell and spatial profiling of sonic hedgehog medulloblastoma identifies novel therapeutic targets

Abhinav Jain, Bohyeon Yu, Jangham Jung, Emanuele Filiberto Rosatti, Joanna J. Phillips — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13073-026-01803-x

Abstract Background Sonic hedgehog (SHH) medulloblastoma (MB) accounts for approximately one-third of all MB cases, the most common malignant brain tumor in children. Despite therapy, SHH MB relapse is frequent and has a dismal prognosis. New treatments for recurrent SHH MB are needed; however, the molecular drivers of relapse are poorly understood. Methods We profiled 22 SHH MB specimens (16 primary and 6 recurrent), including four primary–recurrent patient pairs, using overlapping single-nucleus RNA sequencing (snRNA-seq; n = 16), single-nucleus assay for transposase-accessible chromatin sequencing (snATAC-seq; n = 13), and spatial transcriptomic ( n = 6) cohorts. We integrated these data with exome-derived variant calls from 25 specimens, including seven patients with primary and recurrent samples. Bulk RNA-seq data from 48 specimens representing 24 primary–recurrent pairs were used for validation. Results Our analyses show evidence of positive selection for nonsynonymous tumor mutations, including TP53 mutations involving its DNA-binding and tetramerization domains. TP53 mutations were associated with reduced base-excision repair (BER) gene expression and accessibility of linked cis-regulatory regions. Recurrent tumors showed increased tumor mutational burden (TMB) and an endoplasmic-reticulum (ER) stress expression program associated with poorer survival. Angiogenic gene expression and inferred immunosuppressive myeloid signaling were increased in recurrent disease. Conclusions These data define recurrence-associated cellular and regulatory programs, nominate candidate therapeutic targets and prognostic markers, and provide a single-cell and spatial atlas of SHH MB.

Myeloid-derived IL6/JAK/STAT3 signaling establishes a spatially organized immunosuppressive niche to fuel colitis-associated carcinogenesis

Yuehong Chen, Shunyi Wang, Tingyu Mou, Jun Luo, Mingzhen Cheng — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12967-026-09050-9

Chronic inflammation drives the malignant transformation of colitis, yet the underlying mechanisms remain incompletely understood. IL6/JAK/STAT3 axis has been implicated in inflammation-associated cancer, but its precise cellular sources, spatial organization, and functional dynamics during colitis-associated carcinogenesis (CAC) require systematic investigation. DSS-induced colitis and AOM/DSS-induced CAC models were established in wild-type and Apc Min/+ mice, assessed with survival and histological analysis. Multi-omics approaches, including bulk RNA-seq ( n = 5 samples per group), time-series RNA-seq (3 per time point per group), single-cell RNA-seq (3 per group), and spatial transcriptomics (1 per group), were employed to profile transcriptomic kinetics and cellular heterogeneity. IL6 knockout mice were utilized to validate the functional role of IL6. Colonoscopy, histology, and immune infiltration analyses were performed to assess disease severity and tumor burden. Single-cell transcriptomic profiling reveals a coordinated remodeling of the immune landscape during CAC progression, characterized by M2 macrophage polarization and expansion of regulatory T and B cell subsets. IL6 exhibits stage-dependent duality. It is essential for mucosal protection during acute colitis, yet its sustained expression from M2-polarized macrophages and stromal cells in the chronic phase drives persistent JAK/STAT3 activation. Spatial transcriptomics identifies a distinct myeloid-enriched niche co-localized with proliferative epithelium, physically coupling IL6 sources with tumor-initiating compartments. IL6 ablation in mice exacerbates acute colitis but paradoxically attenuates tumor development in CAC, confirming its pathogenic role in carcinogenesis. These findings establish the myeloid-derived IL6/JAK/STAT3 axis as a major regulator that orchestrates an immunosuppressive microenvironment and fuels the transition from colitis to colorectal cancer. This axis represents a potential therapeutic target for preventing malignant progression in inflammatory bowel disease.

A pan-transcriptome of cultivated peach (Prunus persica) and its wild relatives

Jiao Lu, Kunlongwen Ma, Cai ZhiXiang, Zhijun Shen, Yanna Shi — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-026-08546-9

Peach ( Prunus persica L.) is an important Rosaceae fruit crop and a model species for Prunus research. Although a peach graph pangenome has recently been reported, transcript diversity across cultivated peach and its wild relatives remains incompletely captured by a single reference annotation or by unspliced pangenome graphs. Here, we describe a peach pan-transcriptome resource built from 477 RNA-seq libraries representing 224 accessions, together with support from 217 whole-genome sequencing datasets. The dataset covers cultivated peach and four wild relatives: P. mira , P. davidiana , P. kansuensis and P. ferganensis . After de novo assembly, redundancy reduction, contaminant filtering, reference-based classification and genome-read support filtering, we obtained a non-redundant pan-transcriptome containing 29,622 genes, including 26,871 reference genes, 2,218 unannotated genes and 533 non-reference genes. BUSCO evaluation, read-support filtering and PCR amplification of selected genes were used for technical validation. This resource provides a practical dataset for comparative transcriptomics, presence/absence variation analysis and annotation improvement in peach.

The enhancer-state landscape of breast cancer subtypes: an integrative epigenomic map of regulatory architecture and clinical associations

Lei Yu, Zhizhou Xu, Weichao Li, Tong Yu, Deng Wu — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12964-026-03290-4

Breast cancer (BRCA) is a highly heterogeneous malignancy comprising multiple molecular subtypes with distinct transcriptional and clinical characteristics. Although subtype-specific transcription factors have been well characterized, the enhancer-state architecture underlying subtype identity remains incompletely understood. We performed an integrative multi-omics analysis of breast cancer cell lines and patient datasets by combining histone-mark ChIP-seq, ATAC-seq, DNase-seq, GRO-seq, bulk and single-cell RNA-seq, and clinical transcriptomic data. Enhancers were classified into active, poised, and inactive states based on histone modifications and chromatin accessibility. Subtype-specific enhancers, enhancer-associated genes, transcription factor networks, clinical associations, and responses to BET inhibition were systematically evaluated. We constructed a comprehensive enhancer-state landscape across Basal-like, Her2-enriched, Luminal A, and Luminal B-like breast cancer subtypes. Active enhancers showed stronger subtype-discriminatory power than promoter-associated chromatin features and were closely linked to subtype-specific gene expression programs. Luminal enhancers were enriched for ESR1, FOXA1, and GATA3, whereas Basal and Her2 enhancers were associated with distinct transcription factor signatures, including STAT3, SOX10, MYC, and AP-1. Integration with clinical datasets identified enhancer-associated genes, including TGFB2, TGFB3, INPP4B, and BRD4, with subtype-associated prognostic relevance. Analysis of JQ1-treated MCF-7 cells further suggested that enhancer-associated transcriptional programs may be sensitive to BET inhibition. This study delineates the enhancer-state landscape of breast cancer subtypes and identifies associations among subtype-specific enhancer patterns, transcription factor signatures, gene expression programs, and selected clinical features. These findings provide an integrative epigenomic framework for characterizing breast cancer subtype-associated regulatory patterns and prioritize candidate enhancer-associated relationships for future functional validation.

The Role of Immunohistochemical Markers in the Differential Diagnosis of Lichen Simplex Chronicus

A. S. Sidikov, G.B. Pyagay, Адхамжон Пакирдинов, Ashurov Dilshod Davlatovich — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.17816/dv717256

BACKGROUND: Lichen simplex chronicus (Vidal's lichen) is one of the most common chronic pruritic dermatoses, characterized by a persistent itch–scratch cycle and the development of marked lichenification. Despite its high prevalence, the immunopathogenic mechanisms underlying the disease remain incompletely understood, and the immunohistochemical criteria for its differential diagnosis from atopic dermatitis and psoriasis require further clarification.AIM: To investigate the immunohistochemical expression patterns of cellular markers in patients with lichen simplex chronicus and to determine their diagnostic significance in comparison with atopic dermatitis and psoriasis.METHODS: An immunohistochemical analysis was performed on skin biopsy specimens obtained from 60 patients with lichen simplex chronicus, 30 patients with atopic dermatitis, 30 patients with stable plaque psoriasis, and 10 samples of normal skin from healthy controls. The expression of CD4, CD8, CD1a, CD68, CD207 (Langerin), and S100 was evaluated in both the epidermis and dermis, followed by comparative statistical analysis.RESULTS: Patients with lichen simplex chronicus demonstrated pronounced dermal infiltration by CD4⁺ and CD8⁺ lymphocytes, indicating chronic T-cell–mediated inflammation. CD1a expression confirmed the activation of epidermal Langerhans cells, whereas strong CD68 expression suggested a substantial role of macrophages in sustaining chronic inflammation and tissue remodeling. Statistically significant differences in the expression of CD4, CD8, CD1a, and CD207 among the study groups confirmed their diagnostic value. The limited expression of S100 indicated the absence of significant structural alterations of nerve fibers and supported the predominantly functional nature of chronic pruritus.CONCLUSION: Lichen simplex chronicus is characterized by chronic T-cell– and macrophage-mediated inflammation accompanied by activation of Langerhans cells and dermal dendritic cells, in the absence of significant structural damage to nerve fibers. These immunohistochemical findings improve the accuracy of the differential diagnosis of lichen simplex chronicus from atopic dermatitis and psoriasis and provide further insight into the immunopathogenesis of this dermatosis.

Shotgun metagenomic and untargeted metabolomic analysis of gut microbiome associated with peripheral artery disease

Sarbjeet Niraula, Jae Woong Jang, Jon Jung, Spencer L. Stirewalt, Megan E. Alagna — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.16.757428

Background: While the human gut microbiome is a potentially important and modifiable risk factor for cardiovascular diseases, the links between the gut microbiome and peripheral artery disease (PAD) and its clinical subgroups (claudication and chronic limb-threatening ischemia; CLTI) are not well understood. Methods: We used shotgun metagenomics and untargeted metabolomics to analyze the fecal samples of people with PAD and age- and sex-matched non-PAD controls collected in a cross-sectional manner. We used functional prediction pathways, a network analysis, and weighted gene co-expression network analysis of metagenomic, metabolomics, and clinical data to better understand PAD-associated dysbiosis. Results: Alpha and beta diversity were similar between the PAD (n=78) and non-PAD (n=20) groups. Differentially abundant species were dominated by those belonging to the Lachnospiraceae, Ruminococcaceae and Enterobacteriaceae families. Fecal butyrate levels were reduced between the non-PAD and PAD groups, but differences were not statistically significant. There was significantly reduced alpha diversity in the samples of the CLTI compared to claudication subgroup. Abundance of multiple butyrate producers were greater in the claudication group compared to CLTI. Network analysis revealed 5 meta-modules, each with specific associations to PAD presentation and metabolomic profiles, and species that are both top module hubs and differentially abundant between groups. Community interconnectedness appears to increase in claudication, followed by community disintegration in CLTI. Conclusions: This metagenomic-metabolomic analysis of the gut microbiome of individuals with PAD, highlights the importance of studying functional capacity, diversity, and connectivity between species. Microbial modules may allow an integrative view of the microbial ecology relevant to presence and progression of PAD.

Integrated bioinformatic analysis identifies distinct neddylation-associated proteasome genes and immune-related features in laryngeal squamous cell carcinoma

Shujuan Wang, Jing Xu, Hong Ge — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-76451-7

Neddylation contributes to cancer progression, but its relationship with proteasome-related genes and the immune microenvironment in laryngeal squamous cell carcinoma (LSCC) remains unclear. We aimed to identify neddylation-associated genes in LSCC and characterize their molecular, metabolic, and immune features. Transcriptomic data from The Cancer Genome Atlas and Gene Expression Omnibus were analyzed using differential expression, functional enrichment, and machine-learning approaches. Key genes were further evaluated by survival, pathway, immune infiltration, and somatic mutation analyses and validated by quantitative real-time PCR (qRT-PCR) in five paired LSCC and adjacent tissues. Seventy-four differentially expressed neddylation-related genes were identified. Four genes, KCTD6, PSMB2, PSMB4, and PSMD2, were selected by both LASSO and SVM-RFE. We identified a distinct set of proteasome-related neddylation-associated genes associated with metabolic and immune characteristics in LSCC. These findings suggest potential links between neddylation-associated proteasome genes and the immune microenvironment in LSCC.

Analysis scripts for an independent-cohort audit of the LSC subtype framework for venetoclax response in AML

Shuai Zhang — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23438535

Independent-cohort reproducibility companion to a Correspondence submitted to the American Journal of Hematology ("Independent-cohort audit of the LSC subtype framework for venetoclax response in AML: LMPP and monocytic associations replicate, MEP does not"). A recent framework (Waclawiczek et al., Cell Stem Cell 2026) proposed that venetoclax (VEN) sensitivity in AML is determined by leukemic stem cell (LSC) subtype: LMPP-LSC (BCL2-high) = sensitive, MEP-LSC (BCL-xL-high) = resistant, MoDe-LSC (MCL1-high) = resistant. We tested whether this framework replicates in an independent cohort (Beat AML, n=184 primary; n=179 signature projection). This is a replication audit, not a discovery study. This deposit contains the three analysis scripts that execute the frozen audit protocols:- run_strata.py — primary endpoints P1-P6 and predefined strata (TP53 / RAS / monocytic quartiles); Spearman + bootstrap 2000, fixed seed 20261015; writes M3_beataml_strata_results.json.- run_signature_projection.py — independent signature-projection re-test of the four published subtype signatures; writes M3_v2_signature_projection.json.- run_panoptosis_projection.py — exploratory PANoptosis / BCL2-family analyses; writes M3_v3_panoptosis_projection.json. The frozen protocols themselves (v1.0-v3.0, including adjudication thresholds and the pre-freeze exploratory-analysis disclosure) are provided in the Supplementary Material of the manuscript, in English. Script comments are in Chinese, the authors' working language. Scripts are pure Python (numpy + pandas) and read input data from a local directory (hard-coded BASE path at the top of each script; adjust to your local path before running). Input data are publicly available: the BeatAML2 harmonized release (biodev/beataml2.0_data, CC-BY-4.0) and CIBERSORTx deconvolution results distributed via the original authors' repository (https://github.com/amleppa/vv-resistance_paper). Honesty disclosure: a round of exploratory pre-analysis preceded the protocol freeze and was downgraded to exploratory; all confirmatory conclusions derive exclusively from post-freeze analyses (stated in the manuscript limitations).

QuantDSF: an Open-Source Platform for Comprehensive Nano Differential Scanning Fluorimetry Analysis

Shuo Zhou, Yaxia Yuan, Jyoti Vishwakarma, Dong Zhu, Reuben S. Harris — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.analchem.6c03505

Abstract Nano differential scanning fluorimetry (nanoDSF) is widely used for label-free protein stability and ligand binding analysis, yet most workflows reduce each experiment to a single melting temperature (Tm), discarding the native-state fluorescence, concentration dependence, and temperature-resolved binding information present in the raw data. Here, we present QuantDSF, an open-source, fully transparent platform that converts standard concentration-series nanoDSF experiments into three layers of quantitative information typically obtained from separate assays: thermal stability, affinity, and binding thermodynamics. From a single experiment, QuantDSF reports Tm and ligand-induced shifts, legacy 4PL midpoints (Cmid) with method-aware quality control, and apparent ΔH and ΔS via isothermal slicing and Van’t Hoff regression. Tm is determined through three complementary algorithms, letting users match the method to their data. Native-state static fluorescence quenching and Gaussian deconvolution of overlapping transitions further capture binding events that Tm-only analyses miss. We validate QuantDSF on small molecules, nucleic acids, metal ions, and lipids, with orthogonal validation against isothermal titration calorimetry and surface plasmon resonance. In addition, QuantDSF enables label-free characterization of proteolysis-targeting chimera (PROTAC)−induced ternary complex formation across both VHL and CRBN E3 platforms. We further show that QuantDSF resolves the BCL-xL versus BCL-2 selectivity of DT2216, a BCL-xL−selective PROTAC, that proximity-based assays miss because it reports the thermodynamic stability of the ternary complex rather than spatial proximity alone. By exposing the full analytical content of nanoDSF data through reproducible open-source workflows, QuantDSF extends nanoDSF from endpoint stability screening into a quantitative platform for protein−ligand binding, thermodynamic analysis, and ternary complex characterization.

Nature based strategies for heat and drought resilience in fruit crops through genotype environment and management interactions

Kushagra Sharma — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44372-026-00941-1

Abstract Climate change increasingly threatens fruit crop productivity by disrupting physiological, biochemical, and cellular processes that govern heat and drought tolerance. Yet efficacy of proposed interventions varies widely and is rarely explained by a unifying conceptual framework. Existing literature largely evaluates microbial inoculants, biostimulants, phytohormonal agents, and physical stress-mitigation strategies in isolation, obscuring their mechanistic overlap and the reasons for inconsistent field performance. This narrative review addresses that gap by synthesizing peer-reviewed literature within a genotype × environment × management (G×E×M) framework, examining plant growth-promoting rhizobacteria, arbuscular mycorrhizal fungi, synthetic microbial consortia, non-microbial biostimulants, phytohormonal priming agents, and antitranspirants alongside agroecological and crop-specific case studies. The synthesis indicates that these interventions converge on shared redox, hormonal, and osmotic regulatory networks, and that integrated, context-specific approaches consistently outperform single interventions in improving photosynthetic performance, water-use efficiency, and fruit yield and quality under stress. However, translation to commercial orchards remains constrained by microbiome instability, genotype-dependent responses, formulation and delivery limitations, and the absence of standardized multi-environment validation. Future progress requires predictive, systems-based research integrating multi-omics profiling, microbiome engineering, precision management, and improved delivery formulations to enable scalable, evidence-based climate-resilient fruit production. As a narrative rather than systematic review, this synthesis provides a conceptual foundation for guiding future predictive modelling and field validation.

Integrative multi-omics analysis implicates RSAD2-associated CXCL1–CXCR1/2 signaling in tumor–macrophage communication and progression of endometrial cancer

Genyi Jiang, Yiqin Zhang, Yan Ding, Minyue Cao, Luyao Kang — 2026-10-17 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12967-026-09080-3

Immunotherapy in endometrial cancer (EC) faces challenges due to patient-specific heterogeneity and biomarkers linking tumor-intrinsic programs to the tumor microenvironment remain limited. This study aimed to identify immune-related prognostic factors and investigate the role of RSAD2 in EC progression and tumor–macrophage communication. Integrative multi-omics analyses of TCGA-UCEC were used to construct a prognostic signature and prioritize Radical S-adenosyl methionine domain-containing protein 2 (RSAD2) for further investigation. The functional effects of RSAD2 were evaluated using gain- and loss-of-function assays and a xenograft model. Bulk RNA sequencing, public single-cell RNA sequencing, qRT-PCR, ELISA, and macrophage conditioned-medium and co-culture assays with the CXCR1/2 inhibitor SX-517 were performed to explore RSAD2-associated signaling. Clinical relevance was assessed by immunohistochemistry, multiplex immunohistochemistry, and survival analyses in a retrospective EC cohort ( n = 254). The multi-omics score stratified TCGA-UCEC patients according to survival and distinct immune features, and RSAD2 showed an adverse survival association with macrophage-related immune signatures. RSAD2 overexpression enhanced EC-cell proliferation, migration, invasion, colony and sphere formation, and xenograft growth, whereas RSAD2 knockdown produced the opposite effects. Transcriptomic and biochemical analyses associated RSAD2 with RAF–MEK–ERK activation and increased CXCL1 expression, and CXCL1 secretion changed concordantly with RSAD2 expression under inflammatory stimulation. Conditioned medium from RSAD2-overexpressing EC cells enhanced THP-1-derived macrophage chemotaxis and induced a mixed activation-associated transcriptional response, which was attenuated by CXCR1/2 inhibition. In the clinical cohort, RSAD2 expression was increased in EC tissues and was associated with immune-cell infiltration and poorer survival outcomes in univariable analyses. RSAD2 is a candidate biomarker of EC progression. Our findings support a potential role for RSAD2 associated CXCL1–CXCR1/2 signaling in tumor–macrophage communication, although further ligand-specific, genetic, and immune-competent validation is required to establish its mechanistic and translational significance.
