Upgrading the HIV vaccine paradigm: accounting for host-pathogen diversity through geotargeting-based vaccine design

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Luciano Lunardi, Alexandra de Azevedo da Rocha, Ana Carolina Alves de Oliveira, Iuri Marques de Oliveira, Angelica Cerveira De Baumont — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fcimb.2026.1927865

Introduction Effective HIV vaccine development, with satisfactory levels of response and coverage, remains an elusive goal in immunology. In this context, considerations of host-pathogen interactions from a geotargeting perspective could enhance disease control efforts. Current HIV vaccine strategies increasingly seek to address viral diversity, but the combined effects of regional viral variation and host HLA diversity on antigen processing and presentation remain insufficiently considered. Here, we evaluated whether integrating these host–pathogen variables could support geographically informed prioritization of HIV vaccine targets. Methods Using NetMHCpan 4.1, NetChop 3.1, and the IEDB antigen-processing prediction tool, we evaluated antigen-processing and presentation steps for HIV epitopes associated with viral control. We recovered Human Leukocyte Antigen (HLA) alleles associated with HIV control and their frequencies, and the protein sequences for the most common HIV subtypes in different African countries. We created maps linking both sets of information for each specific region. We then predicted the impact of mutations associated with different HIV strain sequences on proteasome processing score, TAP transportation and HLA binding affinity for the alleles in each host population. Results The generated maps provide a landscape of the impact of genetic diversity on T cell responses for each examined epitope target. TW10 epitope variants in the main HIV subtypes showed consistent HLA binding, proteasome cleavage and processing rankings across the continent. Conversely, TI8 variants carrying I8T or I8R substitutions lost predicted HLA-B*51:01 binding and showed marked deterioration in the combined processing ranking, from 10th for the reference epitope to positions as low as 97th among the analyzed variants. Discussion The adoption of uniform immunotherapy strategies based solely on reference HIV epitopes may be an inefficient approach. Certain epitopes may be suitable vaccine targets for specific locations, when matching a prevalent HIV strain with a host population with high prevalence of HLA alleles that can effectively display the vaccine’s epitopes. However, HLA matching is not enough if the HIV strains in the region have upstream or downstream mutations that prevent the proper processing and presentation of the targeted viral epitope. Based on this complex host–pathogen interplay, we propose a geotargeting approach to vaccine design, considering customized targets for specific geographical locations and host populations. By incorporating regional viral and HLA diversity into target selection, this strategy may increase the likelihood of developing more effective HIV vaccines, although the resulting predictions will require experimental validation.

Extracellular matrix remodeling and regulated cell-death-immune associations in adhesive capsulitis: a three-cohort transcriptomic analysis

Lin Huang, Ling Cheng, Jin He, Rui Zhang, Xia Li — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fcell.2026.1951682

Background Adhesive capsulitis (AC) is a fibroinflammatory disorder characterized by painful shoulder restriction and progressive capsular fibrosis. Whether regulated cell-death (RCD) programs are consistently altered across independent transcriptomic cohorts remains uncertain. Methods We reanalyzed three public bulk RNA-seq cohorts. GSE140731 was the discovery cohort (22 AC and 26 shoulder-instability surgical controls), and GSE190023 and GSE238053 were independent validation cohorts. Differential expression was estimated from filtered raw counts with PyDESeq2, with age/sex adjustment in GSE140731. Curated pyroptosis, ferroptosis, and necroptosis gene sets; immune marker–gene scores; pathway enrichment; STRING network structure; cross-cohort concordance; an exploratory three-gene classifier; and qPCR validation of four selected genes in TGF-β1-stimulated SW982 cells were evaluated. Results The adjusted discovery analysis identified 439 upregulated and 113 downregulated genes at FDR < 0.05. ECM–receptor interaction was strongly positively enriched (NES = 2.299, q = 0.001), making extracellular-matrix remodeling the clearest pathway-level signal. RCD findings were mixed: pyroptosis was not different (delta = −0.105, q = 0.708), ferroptosis was lower but borderline after FDR correction (delta = −0.426, q = 0.056), and necroptosis was lower (delta = −0.320, q = 0.035); the core necroptosis gene set was also negatively enriched (NES = −1.673, q = 0.038). Conclusion Across the three cohorts, ECM remodeling was the most reproducible feature of AC, whereas RCD- and immune-related signals were cohort-dependent and did not support uniform activation of pyroptosis, ferroptosis, or necroptosis. The classifier and RCD signatures remain hypothesis-generating. High-Resolution Multiscale Seismic Fracture Prediction via Basis Pursuit Frequency Extension-Enhanced Multi-Attribute Fusion

Jun Wang, Junxing Cao, Yajuan Xue — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/rs18203551

Fractures are important storage spaces and flow pathways in tight reservoirs, but small-scale fractures with weak seismic responses are difficult to characterize with band-limited post-stack seismic data. This study proposes a high-resolution multiscale seismic method for predicting fracture distribution based on basis pursuit frequency extension (BPFE)-enhanced multi-attribute fusion. BPFE represents each trace sparsely on a wedge dictionary built from a zero-phase Morlet wavelet, which is derived from the average dominant frequency of the data. It then re-synthesizes the basis pursuit denoising solution with a time-compressed wavelet, thereby extending the effective bandwidth without estimating the seismic wavelet. Dip-steered third-generation coherence and ant-tracking attributes are subsequently normalized and fused with explicit weights according to their attribute-sensitive scales, and objective rules are provided for selecting the BPFE parameters. Synthetic tests with known reflectivity show that BPFE reproduces the ideal high-resolution section with correlation coefficients of 0.978 (noise-free) and 0.882 (signal-to-noise ratio of 10 dB). BPFE increases the dominant frequency from 30.8 to 49.3 Hz and reduces the tuning thickness from 16 to 11 ms without systematic phase rotation. At 10 dB, it also raises the area under the receiver operating characteristic curve for detecting weak bed-confined discontinuities from 0.580 to 0.687, outperforming spectral whitening, spiking deconvolution, inverse-Q filtering and sparse-spike inversion in this respect. Applied to the tight sandstone reservoir in the northeastern Sichuan Basin, the workflow delineates main faults, fault-associated fracture zones and small-scale discontinuities more completely than coherence, ant tracking or their fusion without BPFE, which is consistent with the actual geological understanding. The workflow offers a wavelet-estimation-free approach for characterizing weak-response small-scale fractures, although its generalization requires further field validation.

Physically Constrained Inverse-Gaussian Deconvolution of Diffusion-Broadened Drift-Tube Ion Mobility Spectra

Haoran Huang, Zhicheng Li, Lu Chen, Jianna Yu, Wenjie Liu — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.analchem.6c05780

Abstract Peak overlap limits chemical assignment in drift-tube ion mobility spectrometry (DT-IMS). Diffusion broadening is commonly modeled with symmetric Gaussian kernels, although a fixed detector records ion first-passage times. We developed an inverse-Gaussian-Nernst-Townsend-Einstein (IG-NTE) deconvolution framework in which the point-spread function (PSF) follows an inverse-Gaussian distribution and its temporal width is constrained by instrument settings through the NTE relation. The resulting drift-time-dependent measurement operator was inverted by nonnegative Richardson-Lucy iteration without peak-width calibration or specification of component number. In simulations, relative to a Gaussian kernel, IG-NTE deconvolution reduced the average full-residual root-mean-square value by 11.4%, the right-tail residual by 18.1%, and the mean absolute peak-area deviation from 5.10% to 1.19%. Applied to a 63Ni DT-IMS instrument, the method separated ethyl 2-methylpentanoate and 1,8-cineole responses 0.040 ms apart while retaining 2,6-di-tert-butylpyridine (DTBP) as a single-component control. It also recovered paired alkyl-pyrazine mobility features consistent with protonation site- and hydration-dependent collision cross sections. In the negative mode, dichloromethane-derived chloride responses assigned to 35Cl− and 37Cl− exhibited a 3.08:1 area ratio and were supported by GC-TOF-MS. These results show that detector-domain transport modeling can improve the effective reconstructed resolution of diffusion-broadened DT-IMS spectra without hardware modification, provided that controls and forward residual analysis are used to assess reconstruction artifacts.

Cross-Platform Evaluation of a Tumor-Budding-Associated Gene Signature in Colorectal Cancer.

Pritom Sarkar, Prapthi Sarkar, Prapthi Sarkar, Prapthi Sarkar — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.09.757976

Spatial transcriptomics has identified AIF1, LIF, MRC1, and TNC as enriched in tumor budding (TB) regions of colorectal cancer. We investigated whether this TB associated gene module could be detected in bulk transcriptomic data. Pairwise gene co-occurrence was analyzed in bulk RNA-seq (TCGA-COADREAD, n=547) and single-cell RNA-seq (GSE178341, n=26,057 cells) using Fisher's exact tests with Benjamini-Hochberg correction. In bulk RNA-seq, pairwise fold-enrichments were weak (1.16 to 2.72), with AIF1 to LIF showing no significant association (FE=1.16, FDR=0.31). In single-cell data, the same pairs showed 31 to 184.5 fold enrichment (all FDR < 8e-38). Total UMI counts provided no evidence of doublet artifacts explaining these co-detection patterns. Exploratory enhancer-promoter interaction analysis in HCT116 cells further suggested these genes are controlled differently. GOT1 and MDH1, two malate-aspartate shuttle genes, showed lower expression in late-stage CRC (FDR less than or equal to 0.010). A bulk-derived risk score showed a modest association (Cox HR=1.21, p=0.021). Overall, TB-associated co-expression was more clearly detected at single-cell than bulk resolution, highlighting the importance of transcriptomic resolution when studying tumor-microenvironment-associated gene signatures.

Single-cell profiling of physically interacting immune cells using image-guided sorting

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Ningxin Kang, Cheryl Kim, Thomas J. Scriba, Bjoern Peters, Julie G. Burel — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.09.758094

Single-cell transcriptomics of cell-cell complexes offers key insights into in vivo immune interactions, yet distinguishing true physical contacts from artifacts remains challenging. Here, we integrated spectral imaging cell sorting with physical cell deconvolution to separate circulating T cell-monocyte doublets from tuberculosis patients into physically interacting (in-contact) and non-interacting (coincidental) populations. Single-cell sequencing of individual cell partners revealed that physical contact is associated with robust signatures distinct from singlets, including unidirectional monocyte-to-T cell RNA transfer, heightened metabolic activity and T cell cytotoxicity, and a trend toward antigen-specific enrichment. These signatures spanned all T cell and monocyte subpopulations, reflecting a global program of T cell-monocyte engagement. Coincidental doublets displayed intermediate transcriptional profiles between singlet and in-contact states, suggesting the presence of recently disrupted or forming physical pairs. This framework provides high-fidelity deconvolution of cellular interactions, uncovering signatures governing T cell-monocyte communication in human disease.

Integrating phytometabolomics, Drosophila proteomics, and network pharmacology to evaluate the metabolic effects of Citrus maxima peel extract following bisphenol a and benzyl butyl phthalate exposure

Woorawee Inthachat, Pornsiri Pitchakarn, Sataporn Phochantachinda, Tanongsak Laowanitwattana, Pensiri Buacheen — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jff.2026.107546

Bisphenol A (BPA) and benzyl butyl phthalate (BBP) are endocrine-disrupting chemicals (EDCs) with reported obesogenic activity, attracting interest in their potential contribution to obesity and metabolic dysfunction. This study investigated the effect of pomelo peel extract (PPE) on BPA- or BBP-associated metabolic alterations in 3T3-L1 adipocytes and Drosophila using an integrated multi-omics approach. HPLC-qTOF-MS profiling revealed prominent negative-ion signals tentatively annotated as naringin. BBP caused greater disruption of adipocytes than BPA, with ∼4-fold higher accumulation of lipid droplets. PPE did not significantly reduce total lipid-droplet area but altered lipid-droplet morphology. In Drosophila , BPA and BBP decreased survival, disturbed carbohydrate homeostasis, and impaired locomotion. PPE improved survival and metabolic homeostasis and were comparable to pioglitazone. Proteomics identified distinct sets of proteins with significant overall abundance differences among treatment groups in the BPA and BBP datasets, with mitochondrial pathways represented in both. Importantly, PPE co-treatment was associated with a distinct proteomic profile, suggestive of an adaptive and stress-responsive network remodeling, rather than complete restoration toward control. To summarize, PPE seems to have a potential effect against EDC-induced metabolic dysfunction and support sustainable pomelo peel valorization.

Integrating Abiotic Stress Adaptation and Disease Resistance in Climate-Resilient Crops: Pangenomics, Genomic Selection, and AI-Driven Breeding

Yousol Hwang, Gwangmin Ryu, Moon‐Sub Lee, Yoon‐Sup So, Ju‐Kyung Yu — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/plants15203171

Climate change is increasing the frequency and intensity of abiotic stresses, including drought, high temperature, salinity, and waterlogging, which not only directly constrain crop growth and productivity but also reshape plant–pathogen interactions by altering host physiology and immune responses. These climate-driven environmental pressures can accelerate pathogen spread and generational turnover while compromising crop disease resistance through complex interactions between abiotic stress responses and plant immunity. Concurrently, modern cultivars have lost a substantial portion of their genetic diversity through prolonged productivity-focused selection, limiting their capacity to respond to rapidly changing environmental and disease pressures. Consequently, resistance strategies relying primarily on a few major resistance genes and Marker-Assisted Selection (MAS) are becoming increasingly insufficient for maintaining stable crop performance under climate change. This review discusses next-generation strategies for developing climate-resilient disease-resistant crops through three complementary approaches. First, we examine quantitative disease resistance (QDR), which is typically governed by multiple loci with small to moderate effects and can contribute to durable resistance, although its effectiveness depends on the host genotype, pathogen isolate, and environment. We also evaluate the application of genomic selection (GS) to predict quantitative resistance and select individuals with favorable genomic estimated breeding values across major crops. Third, we highlight AI-driven precision breeding that integrates genomic, transcriptomic, metabolomic, phenomic, and environmental information to decipher complex genotype-by-environment (G × E) interactions and improve the prediction of stress-associated disease resistance. Collectively, integrating genetic diversity, genome-wide prediction, multi-omics, and AI provides a framework for breeding toward durable disease resistance and stable productivity under increasingly complex abiotic stress environments, with resistance performance requiring validation across relevant pathogen isolates and environments.

INFLAMMATION AND IMMUNE-MEDIATED DISEASES IN COMPANION ANIMALS: PATHOLOGICAL MECHANISMS, DIAGNOSTIC APPROACHES AND EMERGING PERSPECTIVES

Nancy Bajaj, Anushri Tiwari, Prateek Mishra, Drishti Jain, Surya Gond — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23417261

Inflammation is a crucial defense mechanism against infection, tissue damage, and other harmful stimuli. However, persistent or dysregulated inflammation can lead to tissue damage and immune-mediated disease. Significant inflammatory and immune-mediated disorders in dogs and cats include immune-mediated hemolytic anemia (IMHA), immune-mediated thrombocytopenia, immune-mediated polyarthritis, chronic inflammatory enteropathies, dermatological conditions, renal disease, and inflammatory disorders of the nervous system. These conditions include complex interactions between innate and adaptive immunity, cytokines, genetic susceptibility, environmental factors, and the microbiome. Histopathology is an essential diagnostic method even if immunohistochemistry, molecular diagnostics, and inflammatory biomarkers provide additional information. Emerging technologies like digital pathology, artificial intelligence, spatial biology, and multi-omics may improve future treatment strategies and disease characterization.

Nanopore Sequencing for Epigenetic Decoding: Applications, Analytical Constraints, and Translational Prospects

Jiachao Xin, Jiaxuan Liu, Yichao Liu, Xinru Liu, Mengke Ma — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms27209175

Epigenetic regulation involves multiple molecular layers, including DNA and RNA modifications, histone post-translational modifications (PTMs), and chromatin accessibility. These processes contribute to cell identity, development, and disease, but their measurement remains technically challenging because sequence context, molecular modification, chromatin state, and transcript structure are often assayed separately. As a representative third-generation sequencing technology, nanopore sequencing is profoundly reshaping the interpretation of the epigenetic code through its distinctive capabilities, including long-read sequencing, direct single-molecule signal detection, and the identification of nucleic acid modifications without bisulfite conversion or PCR in appropriate workflows. This review summarizes applications of nanopore sequencing in epigenomics and epitranscriptomics and evaluates emerging nanopore-based approaches for protein and histone sensing. We focus on what can currently be measured, the experimental and computational requirements for reliable decoding, and the limitations that must be addressed before broader multi-omic and clinical use.

Evolutionary Diversification and Functional Roles of MYB Transcription Factors in Algae: Insights from Genomic Distribution and Regulatory Networks

Yurong Wang, Chunli Luo, Xunmeng Li, Yulin Liang, Peipei Xu — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/biology15201865

MYB transcription factors (TFs) represent a conserved family of DNA-binding regulators involved in diverse cellular and physiological processes. MYB TFs are well-characterized in terrestrial plants, but their evolutionary history and functional diversity in algae remain poorly understood. This review summarizes current knowledge of the structural classification, evolutionary characteristics, and regulatory functions of algal MYB TFs. Bibliometric analysis of Web of Science Core Collection publications (1 January 2003–27 April 2026) reveals a marked expansion in algal MYB research since 2019, with emphasis on transcriptional regulation, lipid metabolism, and stress adaptation. Phylogenetic analyses indicate that algal MYBs exhibit lineage-specific diversification, despite retention of MYB domains conserved with land plants. Functional studies further demonstrate that MYB TFs regulate cell cycle progression, carbon-concentrating mechanisms, lipid biosynthesis, nutrient metabolism, and abiotic stress responses. Nevertheless, most algal MYB genes remain functionally uncharacterized. Integrating multi-omics with genome editing will improve understanding of MYB-mediated regulatory networks and facilitate future applications in algal biotechnology and metabolic engineering.

A neutrophil-specific GSR integrates pattern recognition receptor in pathogenesis of neonatal late-onset sepsis: evidence from artificial intelligence-driven multi-omics and clinical validation

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Ronghua Wang, Jianfang Gai — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s41065-026-00745-z

Abstract Background Neonatal late-onset sepsis (NS) is a life-threatening condition driven by a dysregulated host response to infection, where neutrophil dysfunction plays a pivotal role. Pattern recognition receptors (PRRs), as the frontline sensors of the innate immune system, are increasingly recognized as key regulators of immune cell function, yet their specific connection to neutrophil biology in NS remains largely undefined. Objective This study aimed to decode a novel PRR-neutrophil (PRRN)-associated molecular signature in NS. Methods Bulk transcriptomic data from NS peripheral samples (GSE138712) were analyzed using Limma, WGCNA, and CIBERSORT to identify shared PRRN-related differentially expressed genes (DEGs). Hub genes were narrowed down via PPI network analysis. A diagnostic model was constructed using 132 machine learning algorithms on the training set (GSE69686, NS peripheral blood bulk profile) and validated on an independent set (GSE25504, NS peripheral blood bulk profile) based on PRRN-associated hub genes. SHAP analysis identified the central hub gene in training set. Single-cell RNA sequencing data of sepsis patient peripheral blood(GSE167363) were used to validate its cell-specific distribution, functional trajectory, and regulatory network at spatial and temporal manners via cutting-edge analytical framework. AI-based drug screening (DrugReflector) and molecular docking computationally nominated a candidate therapeutic compound for NS targeting PRRN-associated central hub genes. Finally, NS patient clinical samples were collected for the validation of expression patterns of hub genes. Results We identified PRRN-associated diagnostic and candidate therapeutic models for NS patients and nominated GSR as the PRRN-associated and up-regulated hub gene in NS pathogenesis. Single-cell analysis confirmed that GSR is predominantly expressed in neutrophils within the NS samples and is enriched in pathways related to innate immune response. Conclusion This study systematically defines novel PRRN molecular co-expression patterns in NS, establishing GSR as a neutrophil-centric hub gene. These findings offer a potential diagnostic framework and an exploratory therapeutic target for NS patients, and the candidate compound requires further experimental validation.

From Chronology to Physiology: A Critical Translational Assessment of Artificial Intelligence for Precision Healthspan Measurement

Timur M. Saliev, Prim B. Singh — 2026-10-16 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/ijtm6040045

Healthspan, the period of life free from major chronic disease and disability, is a central geroscience outcome, yet its prediction remains difficult due to its multidimensional and heterogeneous nature. In this review, we synthesize advances in artificial intelligence (AI) for precision healthspan assessment, shifting from chronological age estimates toward physiologically grounded, individualized health trajectory forecasting. We examine supervised, deep, self-supervised, and reinforcement learning approaches, alongside AI-based biomarkers of aging, including epigenetic, proteomic, imaging, and wearable-derived digital biomarkers. We also discuss multimodal AI integrating multi-omic, imaging, and sensor data, explainable AI for clinical translation, and key issues in validation, regulation, ethics, bias, privacy, and equitable access. We critically appraise the level of validation supporting each major technology, distinguish biological-age estimation from true healthspan prediction, and separate predictive performance from demonstrated clinical utility. In this review, we conclude that AI-powered healthspan algorithms hold promise for shifting healthcare from reactive disease management to proactive, personalized aging management, but current evidence remains strongest for prediction and weakest for clinical utility; translation will require rigorous external and prospective validation, standardized outcomes, robust ethical and regulatory frameworks, and equitable implementation.
